					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content"><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr" data-mw-parsoid-version="0.24.0.0-alpha24" data-mw-html-version="2.8.0"><section data-mw-section-id="0" id="mwAQ"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none" about="#mwt1" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Short description","href":"./Template:Short_description"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Intentional killing of trans women"}},"i":0}}]}'>Intentional killing of trans women</div><span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt1"><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_short_description"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Short_description_is_different_from_Wikidata"/></span>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt2" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"See also","href":"./Template:See_also"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Femicide"},"2":{"wt":"Transgender genocide"}},"i":0}}]}'><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1368532237" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt3" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+.mw-empty-elt+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt2" id="mwBA">See also: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Femicide" title="Femicide">Femicide</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_genocide" title="Transgender genocide">Transgender genocide</a></div>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBQ"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Día_Internacional_del_Orgullo_LGBT_de_Buenos_Aires_2023_8.webp" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBg"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Día_Internacional_del_Orgullo_LGBT_de_Buenos_Aires_2023_8.webp" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/D%C3%ADa_Internacional_del_Orgullo_LGBT_de_Buenos_Aires_2023_8.webp/330px-D%C3%ADa_Internacional_del_Orgullo_LGBT_de_Buenos_Aires_2023_8.webp?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/D%C3%ADa_Internacional_del_Orgullo_LGBT_de_Buenos_Aires_2023_8.webp/960px-D%C3%ADa_Internacional_del_Orgullo_LGBT_de_Buenos_Aires_2023_8.webp?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1365" data-file-type="bitmap" height="213" width="319" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBw"/></a><figcaption id="mwCA">Protester at the Plaza de Mayo in Buenos Aires, Argentina holding a placard with the words: "Stop the Travesticides! Transfemicides! Transhomicides!".</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwCQ"><b id="mwCg">Transfemicide</b> or <b id="mwCw">transfeminicide</b> is defined as the killing of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans_woman" title="Trans woman" id="mwDA">trans woman</a> motivated by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transphobia" title="Transphobia" id="mwDQ">transphobic</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misogynistic" title="Misogynistic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwDg">misogynistic</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmisogynistic" title="Transmisogynistic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwDw">transmisogynistic</a> hatred that has its origins in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cissexist" title="Cissexist" class="mw-redirect" id="mwEA">cissexist</a> cultural and political norms.<sup about="#mwt7" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-1" id="mwEQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwEg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwEw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwFA">]</span></span></a></sup> The term is most often used as a subset of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Femicide" title="Femicide" id="mwFQ">femicide</a>, a theoretical and legal concept describing the murder of women and girls by men on the basis of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexism" title="Sexism" id="mwFg">sexist</a> beliefs and norms.<sup about="#mwt10" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_2-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:0-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-2" id="mwFw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwGA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwGQ">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwGg">]</span></span></a></sup> Journalists and academics alike believe the prevalence of transfemicide to be vastly underreported.<sup about="#mwt13" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-3" id="mwGw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwHA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwHQ">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwHg">]</span></span></a></sup> Journalists Emma Landeros and Joel Aguirre argue that, as hate crimes, transfemicides constitute a 'silent epidemic' in Mexico, with many deaths receiving little or no media coverage.<sup about="#mwt16" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwHw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwIA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIQ">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwIw">Under <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_Europe" title="Transgender Europe" id="mwJA">Transgender Europe</a>, or TGEU, the Trans Murder Monitoring project reported 281 trans and gender-diverse people murdered in 2025 alone, with 90% of those killed being trans women. Notably, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_sex_workers" title="Transgender sex workers" id="mwJQ">sex workers</a> comprised 34% of these deaths, with most of the overall deaths occurring in Latin America.<sup about="#mwt19" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwJg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwJw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKA">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwMg">Femicide, understood as the intentional killing of a woman by a man motivated by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misogynistic" title="Misogynistic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwMw">misogynistic</a> hatred, was established by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diana_E._H._Russell" title="Diana E. H. Russell" id="mwNA">Diana Russell</a> and Jane Caputi and later popularized in Latin America by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcela_Lagarde" title="Marcela Lagarde" id="mwNQ">Marcela Lagarde</a>.<sup about="#mwt55" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:2_6-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":2"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:2-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:2-6" id="mwNg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwNw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOA">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOQ">]</span></span></a></sup> Transfemicide, however, is a term generally attributed to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transfeminist" title="Transfeminist" class="mw-redirect" id="mwOg">transfeminist</a> activists in Latin America, who sought to describe the heighted structural violence trans women in the region face by expanding the concept of femicide.<sup about="#mwt58" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:02_7-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":02"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:02-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:02-7" id="mwOw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwPA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt59" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_2-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-2" id="mwPw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwQA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQQ">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQg">]</span></span></a></sup> Academic and assistant professor of Gender and Postcolonial Studies Ana Maria Miranda Mora argues that legislation against femicide, in applying strict interpretations of gender-based violence, has at the same time reproduced violence by making the experiences of trans women and queer cisgender women invisible.<sup about="#mwt62" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-8" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-8" id="mwQw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwRA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwRQ">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwRg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt63" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:2_6-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:2-6" id="mwRw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwSA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSQ">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwSw" aria-labelledby="Legal_views"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Legal_views">Legal views</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Legal views"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwTA">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch" id="mwTQ">Human Rights Watch</a>, in their report on violence against LGBT people in El Salvador, Guatemala, and Honduras, note that Central American governments have long excluded transgender women from preexisting legal structures meant to prosecute femicides. Guatemalan officials have justified this by stating that transgender women are not "biological women" in their eyes and therefore their murders are not covered by such laws.<sup about="#mwt64" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:02_7-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":02"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:02-7" id="mwTg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUA">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwUg">In the case of <i id="mwUw">Vicky Hernández v Honduras,</i> tried before <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-American_Court_of_Human_Rights" title="Inter-American Court of Human Rights" id="mwVA">Inter-American Court of Human Rights</a> (IACHR), the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_F._Kennedy_Human_Rights" title="Robert F. Kennedy Human Rights" id="mwVQ">Robert F. Kennedy Human Rights</a> organization and Red Lésbica Cattrachas successfully argued that the government of Honduras held responsibility for the death of trans activist and sex worker <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicky_Hernández_(murder_victim)" title="Vicky Hernández (murder victim)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwVg">Vicky Hernández</a>. The result of the case was seen as a landmark ruling establishing a powerful precedent for the defense of transgender women and LGBT Latin Americans broadly.<sup about="#mwt67" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-9" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-9" id="mwVw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwWA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWQ">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt70" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-10" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-10"}}'><a href="#cite_note-10" id="mwWw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwXA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXQ">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwXw" aria-labelledby="By_region"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="By_region">By region</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: By region"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwYA" aria-labelledby="North_America"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="North_America">North America</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: North America"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwYQ" aria-labelledby="United_States"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="United_States">United States</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: United States"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt71" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwYg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Further","href":"./Template:Further"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Transgender rights in the United States"},"2":{"wt":"Hate crimes against transgender people in the United States"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt72" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt71" id="mwYw">Further information: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_rights_in_the_United_States" title="Transgender rights in the United States">Transgender rights in the United States</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_crimes_against_transgender_people_in_the_United_States" title="Hate crimes against transgender people in the United States" class="mw-redirect">Hate crimes against transgender people in the United States</a></div>
<p id="mwZA">On December 24, 2024, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago" title="Chicago" id="mwZQ">Chicago</a> Mayor <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandon_Johnson" title="Brandon Johnson" id="mwZg">Brandon Johnson</a> signed an executive order establishing a "Transfemicide Working Group", specifically mentioning Black trans women and the heightened discrimination and violence they face.<sup about="#mwt75" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-11" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-11"}}'><a href="#cite_note-11" id="mwZw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwaA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaQ">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwag">]</span></span></a></sup> In <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Rico" title="Puerto Rico" id="mwaw">Puerto Rico</a>, the 2020 murder of Neulisa Luciano Ruiz, also known as Alexa, sparked outrage among Puerto Rican activists. They saw the police and media misgendering Neulisa as downplaying the transphobic nature of the killing.<sup about="#mwt78" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-12" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-12"}}'><a href="#cite_note-12" id="mwbA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwbQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbg">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbw">]</span></span></a></sup> In response to media coverage of Nuelisa's killing, Puerto Rican rapper <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bad_Bunny" title="Bad Bunny" id="mwcA">Bad Bunny</a> wore a shirt with the words "They killed Alexa, not a man in a skirt" during a performance on <i id="mwcQ">The Tonight Show Starring Jimmy Fallon</i>.<sup about="#mwt81" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-13" id="mwcg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwcw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdA">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwdg" aria-labelledby="Mexico"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Mexico">Mexico</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Mexico"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt82" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwdw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Further","href":"./Template:Further"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Femicide in Mexico"},"2":{"wt":"Transgender rights in Mexico"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt83" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt82" id="mweA">Further information: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Femicide_in_Mexico" title="Femicide in Mexico">Femicide in Mexico</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_rights_in_Mexico" title="Transgender rights in Mexico" class="mw-redirect">Transgender rights in Mexico</a></div>
<p id="mweQ">In Mexico, only five states recognize transfemicide as a distinct legal category, with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayarit" title="Nayarit" id="mweg">Nayarit</a> being the earliest to implement such a law on March 1, 2024.<sup about="#mwt86" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-14" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-14" id="mwew"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwfA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfQ">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfg">]</span></span></a></sup> In 2024, activists successfully pushed the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico_City" title="Mexico City" id="mwfw">Mexico City</a> legislature to legally recognize transfemicides after the 2016 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_Paola_Buenrostro" title="Murder of Paola Buenrostro" id="mwgA">murder of Paola Buenrostro</a>, a trans sex worker. Known as the "Paola Buenrostro law", the legislation makes the killing of a transgender woman a crime punishable by up to 70 years in prison.<sup about="#mwt89" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-15" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-15" id="mwgQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwgg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgw">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwhQ">Mexico has one of the highest rates of transfemicides in the world. Letra Ese, an organization tracking hate crimes, reported 55 transfemicides in 2024, making Mexico one of the deadliest countries in the world for transgender women.<sup about="#mwt92" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-16" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-16" id="mwhg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwhw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiA">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwig" aria-labelledby="South_America"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="South_America">South America</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: South America"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwiw" aria-labelledby="Argentina"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Argentina">Argentina</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Argentina"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwjA"><a href='https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cartel_con_la_consigna_"sBasta_de_Transfemicidios_y_travesticidios".jpg' class="mw-file-description" id="mwjQ"><img resource='https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cartel_con_la_consigna_"sBasta_de_Transfemicidios_y_travesticidios".jpg' src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Cartel_con_la_consigna_%22sBasta_de_Transfemicidios_y_travesticidios%22.jpg/250px-Cartel_con_la_consigna_%22sBasta_de_Transfemicidios_y_travesticidios%22.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Cartel_con_la_consigna_%22sBasta_de_Transfemicidios_y_travesticidios%22.jpg/500px-Cartel_con_la_consigna_%22sBasta_de_Transfemicidios_y_travesticidios%22.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" data-file-type="bitmap" height="167" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwjg"/></a><figcaption id="mwjw">Signage saying "Stop with the transfemicides and travesticides!" at a 2018 protest in response to the transfemicide of Cynthia Moreira in Tucumán, Argentina.</figcaption></figure>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt93" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwkA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Further","href":"./Template:Further"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Transgender rights in Argentina"},"2":{"wt":"LGBTQ rights in Argentina"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt94" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt93" id="mwkQ">Further information: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_rights_in_Argentina" title="Transgender rights in Argentina">Transgender rights in Argentina</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_Argentina" title="LGBTQ rights in Argentina">LGBTQ rights in Argentina</a></div>
<p id="mwkg">In October 2017, Azul Montoro, an Argentinian sex worker, was murdered in the city of Córdoba. In the days after her death, the Asociación de Travestis, Transexuales y Transgéneros de Argentina (Association of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travesti_(gender_identity)" title="Travesti (gender identity)" id="mwkw">Travestis</a>, Transsexuals, and Transgenders of Argentina) or ATTTA, demanded justice for Azul and mobilized protests and candlelight vigils. The perpetrator was eventually found guilty under the nation's femicide law, a historic first for Argentina.<sup about="#mwt97" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-17" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-17"}}'><a href="#cite_note-17" id="mwlA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwlQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlg">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt100" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-18" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-18"}}'><a href="#cite_note-18" id="mwmA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwmQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmg">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwnA" aria-labelledby="Colombia"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Colombia">Colombia</h4><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Colombia"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt101" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwnQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Further","href":"./Template:Further"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Transgender rights in Colombia"},"2":{"wt":"LGBTQ rights in Colombia"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt102" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt101" id="mwng">Further information: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_rights_in_Colombia" title="Transgender rights in Colombia" class="mw-redirect">Transgender rights in Colombia</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_Colombia" title="LGBTQ rights in Colombia">LGBTQ rights in Colombia</a></div>
<p id="mwnw">In <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia" id="mwoA">Colombia</a>, cases of transfemicide are covered under section 104A: "<span title="Spanish-language text" about="#mwt103" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwoQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"es"},"2":{"wt":"Feminicidio"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="es">Feminicidio</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Spanish-language_text" about="#mwt103" id="mwog"/>" (Femicide) of the Colombian legal code.<sup about="#mwt106" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-19" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-19"}}'><a href="#cite_note-19" id="mwow"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwpA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpQ">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpg">]</span></span></a></sup> Specifically, the law states that killings motivated by a [trans] woman's gender-identity, along with the circumstances of the victim's relationship to the perpetrator, constitute femicide.<sup about="#mwt109" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-20" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-20"}}'><a href="#cite_note-20" id="mwpw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwqA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqQ">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqg">]</span></span></a></sup> In 2023, a total of 41 transgender women were killed, many as a result of what are referred to as "social cleansing" campaigns taken on by ultraconservative armed groups in Colombia who seek to terrorize LGBTQ Colombians.<sup about="#mwt112" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-21" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-21"}}'><a href="#cite_note-21" id="mwqw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwrA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrQ">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrg">]</span></span></a></sup> Despite relatively progressive policies regarding gender and sexual minorities, and the existence of the "Feminicidio" Law, many transfemicides continue to go unpunished.<sup about="#mwt115" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-22" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-22"}}'><a href="#cite_note-22" id="mwrw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsQ">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section></section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwsw" aria-labelledby="Activism"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Activism">Activism</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Activism"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mwtA" aria-labelledby="Ni_Una_Menos_(Not_One_Less)"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Ni_Una_Menos_(Not_One_Less)"><span id="Ni_Una_Menos_.28Not_One_Less.29" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Ni Una Menos (Not One Less)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Ni Una Menos (Not One Less)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwtQ"><a href='https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Letrero_de_"Ni_una_menos".jpg' class="mw-file-description" id="mwtg"><img resource='https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Letrero_de_"Ni_una_menos".jpg' src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Letrero_de_%22Ni_una_menos%22.jpg/250px-Letrero_de_%22Ni_una_menos%22.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Letrero_de_%22Ni_una_menos%22.jpg/500px-Letrero_de_%22Ni_una_menos%22.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="5568" data-file-height="3712" data-file-type="bitmap" height="167" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwtw"/></a><figcaption id="mwuA">A set of cans forming the words "Ni una menos" (Not one less) in Argentina.</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwuQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ni_una_menos" title="Ni una menos" id="mwug">Ni Una Menos</a> is a Latin American feminist movement that started in Argentina as a rallying slogan against femicide and misogynistic violence.<sup about="#mwt118" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-23" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-23"}}'><a href="#cite_note-23" id="mwuw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwvA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvQ">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvg">]</span></span></a></sup> Starting as a movement decrying violence against women in general, transgender activists have adopted it to protest against transfemicides. Peruvian activist group Féminas rallied around the slogan "Ni Una Trans Menos" (Not One Trans [Woman] Less) to protest a lack of protections against transphobic violence.<sup about="#mwt121" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-24" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-24"}}'><a href="#cite_note-24" id="mwvw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwwA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwwQ">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwwg">]</span></span></a></sup> In a New York Times interview, feminist theorist and philosopher <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Butler" title="Judith Butler" id="mwww">Judith Butler</a> argues that the Ni Una Menos movement is a broadly inclusive movement in Latin America made up of a diverse group of women, both cisgender and transgender, seeking an end to the killings of people "who are feminized or regarded as feminine".<sup about="#mwt124" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-25" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-25"}}'><a href="#cite_note-25" id="mwxA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwxQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxg">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mwyA" aria-labelledby="Transgender_Day_of_Remembrance_(TDoR)"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Transgender_Day_of_Remembrance_(TDoR)"><span id="Transgender_Day_of_Remembrance_.28TDoR.29" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Transgender Day of Remembrance (TDoR)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Transfemicide&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Transgender Day of Remembrance (TDoR)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwyQ"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Biden-Harris_Transition_TDOR_Graphic.png" class="mw-file-description" id="mwyg"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Biden-Harris_Transition_TDOR_Graphic.png" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Biden-Harris_Transition_TDOR_Graphic.png/250px-Biden-Harris_Transition_TDOR_Graphic.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Biden-Harris_Transition_TDOR_Graphic.png/330px-Biden-Harris_Transition_TDOR_Graphic.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="2048" data-file-type="bitmap" height="141" width="141" class="mw-file-element" id="mwyw"/></a><figcaption id="mwzA">Biden-Harris team in 2020 commemorating Trans Day of Remembrance</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwzQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_Day_of_Remembrance" title="Transgender Day of Remembrance" id="mwzg">Transgender Day of Remembrance</a> (TDoR) is a day of observation celebrated yearly on November 20th memorializing the deaths of transgender people as a result of transphobia. The tradition was started by a small group of transgender people in 1999 who wanted to honor the life of their friend, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Hester" title="Rita Hester" id="mwzw">Rita Hester</a>, who was killed in 1998.<sup about="#mwt127" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-26" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-26"}}'><a href="#cite_note-26" id="mw0A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw0Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0g">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0w">]</span></span></a></sup> In the United States, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_Administration" title="Biden Administration" class="mw-redirect" id="mw1A">Joe Biden administration</a> released yearly press memos commemorating TDoR. The administration's November 20, 2023 release states that, of the 26 transgender Americans killed that year, "the majority of those targeted [were transgender] women of color".<sup about="#mwt130" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-27" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-27"}}'><a href="#cite_note-27" id="mw1Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw1g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1w">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2A">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
